Augsburg homegrown talent Tim Schnitzer seizes opportunity in vital Bundesliga win

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Eighteen-year-old FC Augsburg academy prospect Tim Schnitzer enjoyed an unforgettable afternoon on Sunday, making his first Bundesliga start in his hometown club’s 3-1 victory over Mainz 05. A defensive injury crisis forced head coach Manuel Baum to deploy the youngster in an unfamiliar role, but Schnitzer delivered an impressively composed performance that earned special praise from his trainer.

Speaking to Get German Football News and other journalists in the mixed zone after the match, Schnitzer called it a “dream come true to start where I used to be a ball boy”. The Augsburg native admitted to some nervousness ahead of his starting XI debut. Schnitzer revealed that he had slept less than usual the night before and even awoken before his alarm clock.

“I was definitely a little nervous,” Schnitzer conceded.

The Germany youth international nevertheless settled into his role on the left side of Augsburg’s back-three relatively quickly, displaying maturity and confidence in possession. Schnitzer won 70 percent of his ground challenges and connected with 89 percent of his passes, according to Opta data.

GGFN considered Schnitzer and the rest of the makeshift back-three (consisting of Schnitzer’s fellow out-of-position player Yannik Keitel and young Man City loanee Juma Bah) worthy of above average marks in our player ratings piece.

“When you seize your chance, you become more and more integrated into the team,” Schnitzer remarked in the mixed zone.

Augsburg’s defensive personnel shortage left Baum with little choice but to turn to the 18-year-old, who ordinarily operates as a defensive midfielder. Chrislain Matsima, Jeffrey Gouweleeuw, Noahkai Banks, and Calvin Brackelmann were all unavailable, leaving the returning Juma Bah as the only other natural central defender available to the FCA trainer.

“We were missing four central defenders and were still able to field a back-three,” Baum remarked at the post-match press conference. “And I believe we were able to implement something that we always aim for as a club. Namely, to integrate young players in at the right moment. And we witnessed that with Tim Schnitzer, who delivered a fabulous performance in his first game and the team really helped him out with that.”

“A huge compliment to the whole team,” Baum continued near the end of his opening press conference statement. “And let’s single out Tim again. He’s from our own academy and has been with us since the U9s. I think he grew up just a couple of blocks away from the academy. I think we can be very happy about the result, the team, and the next homegrown talent who was able to start today.”

Schnitzer’s first Bundesliga start represents another significant milestone in a development pathway that Get German Football News has followed closely since the summer. The Augsburg-born midfielder made his senior debut against RB Leipzig last March before spending the summer attempting to establish himself as a genuine first-team option. Schnitzer’s versatility emerged as one of his most promising qualities, with the youngster capable of operating as a defensive midfielder, central defender, or No. 8.

Baum had already identified Schnitzer as one of Augsburg’s most promising academy graduates in August, even mentioning the teenager among several FCA players he hoped might eventually attract Germany senior national-team consideration. Our 2026/27 Augsburg tactical preview also highlighted Schnitzer’s encouraging preseason performances, including his goal against Bournemouth, and suggested that the youngster should have opportunities to collect meaningful Bundesliga minutes.

Sunday’s injury-enforced selection certainly provided one such opportunity. Schnitzer responded by completing the entire 90 minutes and helping Augsburg end their seven-match winless streak. Whether the teenager retains his place once Augsburg’s established defenders return remains to be seen. Baum nevertheless has another option at his disposal, and the youngster who once served as a ball boy at the WWK Arena has taken another important step toward becoming a Bundesliga regular.

GGFN | Peter Weis – reporting from the WWK Arena

Amorim: ‘Milan pace did damage against brave Frosinone’

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Ruben Amorim explains how he made the most of ‘brave’ Frosinone’s approach to run riot in the spaces and focus on the ‘pace’ in his Milan side for a 5-1 win. ‘The team believe in the plan.’

It was a morale-boosting performance from the Rossoneri against a Frosinone side who changed tactics to 3-4-3 with a False 9 for this trip to San Siro.

It left massive gaps in defence that Milan took full advantage of with a counter-attacking masterclass, as after Gabriele Calvani’s own goal, Goncalo Ramos, Ardon Jashari, Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic ran riot.

The only negative was the Patrizio Masini goal to temporarily make it 2-1 going into the half-time break, while Goncalo Ramos went off with a muscular twinge.

“The main thing is that we have a plan in the game, and the team believe in the plan,” Amorim told DAZN Italia.

“You know Frosinone as well as I do, they are all about full pressure, I understood that with a lot of points coming to San Siro, they would be really brave. With the pace we have with Rabiot, Moreira, Pulisic and Ramos, we could make transitions.

“We changed a little bit the way we play the game because of our opponents, and it was a good night for us.”

Goncalo Ramos scored his first goal since October 19, while Jashari hadn’t ever scored in the Milan jersey before this evening.

“We are building principles and understanding the game. We knew Frosinone are about marking man to man, that’s why Pulisic looked like he was in every position, because we knew they would follow us, and we could create a lot of danger with changing roles,” continued Amorim.

“It doesn’t matter who plays, every player in training needs to understand every position on the pitch, so that we can change the characteristics. There are so many things I saw in the game that we need to improve, but we stuck to the plan, and that is something I am pleased with.”

When Ramos went off with a calf twinge, why did Amorim turn to Cisse and not Francesco Camarda?

“You saw the game, we did not spend many minutes around the box, the space was behind. If we were controlling the game in a different way, Camarda was the right way to play, but Cisse is really good with pace. You saw the trouble he made for the opponent was not holding the ball, it was the runs behind, the passes, not a typical striker,” explained the coach.

“I didn’t notice it was Ramos who scored the goal, it was more important that it was 2-0, because Milan need lots of goals to control a game, and also Frosinone had to put more men forward at 2-0, so it opened up more spaces for us.”

Milan have won five of the last six Serie A matches, and consolidated fourth place in the hunt for Champions League qualification.

The 5-3-1 New York Giants are in the pole position to win the NFC East. They're hoping to maintain their first-place cushion in the division on Sunday, searching for a bounce-back victory after falling to the Washington Commanders on "Thursday Night Football" last week.

Fortunately for McCarthy, he's buoyed by a Giants defense that's giving up the third-fewest passing yards per game (184.1) this season.

Follow along with Yahoo Sports for live updates, highlights and more as the Jaguars face the Giants on Sunday afternoon, airing on CBS.

Mauricio Pochettino bemoans ‘unfair’ Liverpool incident in ‘painful’ Manchester United defeat

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino has called Manchester United’s 4-0 defeat by Liverpool “so painful”.

The Argentine, making his bow as the Red Devils’ latest head coach at Anfield, watched on as his new side were thrashed by Andoni Iraola’s hosts in a one-sided affair.

Leny Yoro was dismissed on eight minutes for denying Alexander Isak a clear goalscoring opportunity, and Dominik Szoboszlai, Florian Wirtz, Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz netted the goals that handed the Reds a huge three points, and deservedly so.

Reflecting on the heavy loss, which demonstrated the sheer size of his task ahead, Pochettino bemoaned the red card as a key turning point at such an early moment in the encounter.

"So painful. So disappointed,” the former Tottenham and Chelsea head coach said.

“It's not ok to start a game in the Premier League with this situation. The game changed completely after [eight] minutes. Now we can explain everything but the damage is done, it's so painful.

"The players are so, so feeling the pain of the result. I told them to be calm. It was an unfair situation that happened. We need to accept that reality. We need to try to increase our focus, concentration and make better decisions on the field.

"We also need to be critical of ourselves but some situations happened in the game that we are not happy with."

The 54-year-old identified two reasons why he believed Yoro should not have been sent off, despite VAR validating referee Sam Barrott’s on-field decision to give the French defender his marching orders.

"The ball was for the goalkeeper,” Pochettino explained.

“In that situation, if you review the VAR you can see for sure, he never had the intention to connect with Isak.

“When the control was long with the striker, the ball was going to the goalkeeper, he runs and goes down.

"In Leny's case, it wasn't intentional. It was an unusual situation that is normal, everyone who plays football can feel it.

Mauricio Pochettino suffers unwanted Premier League first in Man Utd loss vs Liverpool

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Saikat Mandal | 22 Nov 2026 19:24

Mauricio Pochettino's Manchester United reign got off to a disastrous start as the Red Devils suffered a humiliating 4-0 defeat against arch-rivals Liverpool at Anfield in the Premier League on Sunday.

The Argentine took charge of the club earlier this week after Man Utd parted ways with Michael Carrick, leaving him with barely a few days to work with his new players on the training ground.

Pochettino had won just three of his previous 19 games before Sunday's encounter, and the final whistle brought no improvement to that record.

The defeat also had a damaging impact on the league standings, with Man Utd dropping to 19th in the Premier League table on eight points after 11 matches, with only Crystal Palace faring worse.

According to Opta Joe, Pochettino became the first manager in Premier League history to see his team concede twice and receive a red card in the first half of his opening match in charge of a club.

The game was only eight minutes old when the Man Utd backline was caught napping, forcing Leny Yoro into a challenge on Alexander Isak that resulted in a red card.

Liverpool capitalised on United's defensive frailties, scoring twice before the break through Dominik Szoboszlai and Florian Wirtz.

The hosts enjoyed greater control after the interval and added two more goals through Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz, with the former also setting a Premier League record of his own.

Pochettino also entered the Man Utd history books for the wrong reasons, suffering the joint-heaviest defeat by any Red Devils manager in his first game in charge, matching Lal Hilditch's 4-0 loss against Bolton Wanderers in October 1926.

Considering Man Utd's struggles so far this season, the defeat against Liverpool need not spell the end of the world for the Red Devils or their new manager.

Pochettino's immediate priority will be to collect as many points as possible and create some breathing space between his side and the teams at the bottom of the table.

There were precious few positives to take from the performance, aside from Senne Lammens's display, but Pochettino will hope to see greater improvement from his players in the coming days.

Liverpool winger Rio Ngumoha aimed a subtle dig at Manchester United by suggesting his side should have scored more than four goals in today’s game.

Ngumoha was in superb form for Liverpool as they beat Man Utd 4-0, and it’s fair to say it was a pretty easy game for the Reds, with plenty of chances for an even bigger score-line.

Speaking after the game, the 18-year-old sounded delighted to shine in this big game, admitting that it’s special to score and assist at such a young age against Liverpool’s rivals.

“Games like Liverpool-United can’t really get much bigger in my eyes. To get the three points here at Anfield is special. It was a really important game today,” Ngumoha said in his post-match interview, as quoted by BBC Sport.

On possibly scoring more against the Red Devils, he added: “Sometimes when we are two goals up we can get a big complacent really. We kept our heads in the game and started controlling it and in the second half we took the game by the scruff of the neck and showed the mentality. We didn’t slack off.

“As fans they always want us to score chances and we know we should have scored more. Creating those chances is important as well. It’s about keeping a cool head and keeping composed and they will come.”

It was far too easy for Liverpool against United today and it means new manager Mauricio Pochettino could really be in for a relegation battle this season.

MUFC are 19th in the table and have won just one game so far, with no clean sheets, and a worse defensive record than any team in Europe’s big five leagues.

Any regrets? Pochettino’s start at Man Utd descends into farce in Anfield rout

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Liverpool fans did not miss a beat. They never do. “Poch is at the wheel,” they goaded as this calamity unfolded. But at the wheel of what, a clown car? The look of thunder on Mauricio Pochettino’s face said it all as he got off to the worst start of any Manchester United manager – or head coach – in 100 years.

The Pochettino bandwagon rolled into town with the usual fanfare of a new era, new belief, new hope; and then the wheels came off, the doors collapsed and the engine exploded. The opening quarter of an hour really was that farcical.

In that time, away to Liverpool of all places, they were 2-0 down, a player down – and down and out. If Pochettino had any uncertainty over the scale of the task ahead of him, there it was laid bare. Any regrets? Whatever the salary, he could be forgiven for having a few as he watched from the touchline.

Maybe Pochettino, too, can take some responsibility. Even this early on. Was it wise to drop Harry Maguire and hand Matthijs de Ligt his first start in 357 days? Not from the way De Ligt was at fault for the first and, arguably, the second goal. Did the head coach start with a back three or a back four? The players seemed confused. It eventually ended up as a back five.

But this was a collective failing for United. From top – with director of football Jason Wilcox caught on camera with his head buried in his coat (he really needs to work on that poker face) – through the club and out on the pitch. Pochettino is big on building a culture. He has to abolish the calamity and the comedy.

The work needed at United is monumental as the inexplicable strategy of rebuilding the attack last year, the midfield in the summer and the defence next year was once again exposed. Whoever did that as a way of building a successful team? Instead United have conceded 34 goals already this season, the most in the top five European leagues.

United will argue, with justification, that Leny Yoro’s eighth-minute sending off was harsh given Alexander Isak appeared to have lost control of the ball. But what was Yoro thinking as he pushed the forward over? And what was De Ligt doing as he watched on, or lumbered on, after failing to track the run?

Leny Yoro is SENT OFF after eight minutes! 😱 https://t.co/VKYvckvBBP

And what were United doing with that shambolic defensive wall, with Bruno Fernandes left waving a leg as Dominik Szoboszlai could not believe his luck at the space he saw? The Liverpool midfielder simply whipped a low free-kick into the corner of the net.

Dominik Szoboszlai SLAMS home the free-kick! 💥 https://t.co/Q09eEi0m0B

The second goal? It highlighted that mismatch as Rio Ngumoha easily beat Diogo Dalot, not for the first and not for the last time, and pulled back a cross that Florian Wirtz hooked in off the crossbar. De Ligt was, quite frankly, all over the place as he again watched on.

It can be argued that, after that disastrous start, Pochettino stemmed the bleeding just as it appeared set to be a bloodbath. But that may also have owed much to a lack of killer instinct from Liverpool, who did not go for the jugular. Still they scored four. It could – should – have been a repeat of the 7-0 from three years ago.

Into the second-half and the chant from the Kop was clear. “Attack, attack, attack,” rang out as they demanded more goals; more humiliation. The wastefulness was highlighted by Bradley Barcola running the ball out of play when through and Isak slicing a shot with only Senne Lammens to beat.

A third goal did come with Barcola easily beating Lisandro Martínez, using his pace down the right, before crossing low for the outstanding Ngumoha to sweep home. “Going down, going down,” sang the Liverpool fans before adding: “You’re not famous anymore”. Not famous but certainly infamous.

Andoni Iraola had a very clear message for his players ahead of this game: don’t fall into a trap.

It’s remarkable to see Manchester United arrive at Anfield in the bottom three but, to go back to that old cliche, the league table will mean nothing when the game begins and Liverpool’s Head Coach knows it only too well.

There will come a point when United leave their desperate form behind and Iraola, who could do with winning this contest after high-profile defeats to Arsenal and Manchester City, Isn’t being kidded by results after 10 games.

“United finished third in the table last season,” Iraola pointed out. “You are looking at a squad with exactly the same players. They had very, very good results, especially in the second half of the season. Now we have just started, this is just 10 games. So, for sure, they will be a lot higher in the table (soon). We have to rate them like this and the truth is the reference we have is that they finished third.”

The key to success for Liverpool will be winning the midfield battle and Dominik Szoboszlai - whose form has been up and down - needs to recapture his best.

It is ironic that in their latest attempt to rejuvenate and restore some of their old fire, Manchester United have turned to a manager whose career needs a similar reawakening.

Having dismissed Michael Carrick so early in the season, United can consider themselves fortunate that a figure of such clout and experience as Mauricio Pochettino was available and willing to leave his role with the United States.

Pochettino can be even more grateful that a club of United’s stature have turned to him in their moment of need.

United need a saviour to first ease them from the relegation zone and then revive their Premier League and Champions League credibility. Pochettino, meanwhile, needed a club like United to save him from a managerial career that promised more than it delivered.

Bruno Fernandes says he has shelved talks over a new contract because it is not the “right moment” for discussions over his future as Manchester United captain.

Fernandes, 32, is out of contract next summer although United have a 12-month option to extend his deal to 2028.

The Portugal midfielder is understood to have rejected two contract offers from United since the summer but said all his focus needs to be on trying to help the team out of their current plight.

United head into Sunday’s game against Liverpool at Anfield second from bottom in the Premier League.

“We spoke about the contract at the end of last season,” Fernandes told Sky Sports.

“We came in at the beginning of this season speaking about the contract, but about the context of the team and the club, I just put that to a side because I don’t think it’s the right moment, neither for me, nor for the club, to be talking about negotiations.

“The most important thing now is that the club gets back to winning games.”

Fernandes said claims he wants more money or a longer-term deal on offer are incorrect and that if finances were the issue he would have left Old Trafford before now. The captain has previously rejected lucrative offers from Saudi Arabia.

“The club offered me a new contract, so I think that, from their side, shows that they want me to stay,” he said.

“There’s no negotiations ongoing, but I’ve seen people saying that ‘He’s asked for more money, he’s asked for more years’. If I wanted more money, I wouldn’t be here.

“I had somewhere to go to get much more money than being at Manchester United. I could have been in a place that is much warmer than Manchester.

“Two summers ago, three summers ago, last summer, I always had the chance to leave and I always chose to be here.

“The thing is, I’ve always showed how much I care about the club and how much I want to win at this club. And I always said, in the moment the club thinks it’s time for me to move on, I will move on.

“I’m not going to be the player that I’ve seen many in this time at Manchester United. They’re going to stay here just by staying, just finish my contract and then we will see. In the moment the club says, ‘Bruno, it’s time to part ways’, I’m going to be the first one walking out the door.”

United chief executive Omar Berrada said of Fernandes on Friday that it was “true that we’re having conversations about his new contract” but that the club preferred “to keep that private”.

Having been voted PFA Player of the Year last season, Fernandes’ form has dropped off this term like that of the team.

He was dropped for the 3-0 defeat by Bournemouth in the wake of a 4-1 drubbing by Como when he criticised the tactics and approach of then head coach Michael Carrick.

Mauricio Pochettino, Carrick’s successor, confirmed on Friday that Fernandes would remain as captain.

Fernandes said the United fans had been “far too patient with us” and hopes Pochettino - who wanted to bring the Portuguese to Spurs in 2019 - will bring a fresh energy.

“He’s someone that likes to bring a very good energy to the team, someone very positive, very strong, strict, said it straight away that he won’t allow us to not give him what he demands from the team, from each player,” Fernandes said.

“He wants the club to succeed so much, he wants to win games, and that’s pretty clear and obvious by the way he and his staff speak and want us to improve.

“Obviously [in 2019] I was a very young player that was eager to come to the Premier League. They had [Harry] Kane there, they had Dele Alli, Son, the chance of playing with those players in front was very attractive.”

Subs: Mamardashvili, Gomez, Chiesa, Tsimikas, Munoz, Frimpong, Nyoni, McConnell, Koumas.

Liverpool 4-0 Man Utd: VAR Expert Casts Verdict on Leny Yoro’s Red Card

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Leny Yoro's sending off in Manchester United's 4-0 defeat to Liverpool came in controversial fashion, and VAR expert Dale Johnson has weighed in on whether referee Sam Barrott got the decision right.

Regardless of the red card, this was a pitiful performance from United and the worst possible start for Mauricio Pochettino. He watched his side capitulate within eight minutes when Matthijs de Ligt, strangely handed a start, got caught out and Yoro raced back before barging into Alexander Isak.

Yoro sent Isak to the turf just outside the box, and Barrott deemed this to have been a red card offence due to the French defender being the last man. Most United fans aren't using the dismissal as an excuse for their team's fifth league defeat of the season, but many are questioning whether the challenge warranted such punishment.

Isak was played through on goal and the Liverpool striker took a heavy touch, sending the ball a fair distance away from him when Yoro made the challenge. VAR didn't feel the need to intervene and potentially downgrade the red card to a caution.

Johnson delved into Barrott's decision and explained that the referee had four key considerations to make over Yoro denying an obvious goalscoring opportunity. These are "the distance between the offence and the goal", "the general direction of play", "the likelihood of keeping or gaining control of the ball" and "the location and number of defenders and attackers".

Three of those factors were satisfied because Isak ran directly towards goal in a central position and United had no covering defender. The main question is the "likelihood of keeping or gaining control of the ball."

United will argue that Yoro's contact came after Isak's touch and therefore there is no case that the challenge caused the player to over-run the ball. Johnson suggested in his BBC Sport article that Barrott and VAR John Brooks had to assess whether Isak would definitely have got to the ball ahead of goalkeeper Senne Lammens.

Johnson added: "And that if he had, it would have been a good scoring chance. If there is any doubt, then it should not be a red card. It is a borderline case, and as such the VAR would not be expected to intervene for a clear and obvious error."

United were put to the sword thereafter when Dominik Szoboszlai scored the opener with the resulting free-kick (10'). Florian Wirtz added a second (13') before Rio Ngumoha (66') and Victor Munoz (83') sealed an emphatic victory for Andoni Iraola's men.

There were differing opinions on the red card amongst Sky Sports pundits as United legends Gary Neville and Roy Keane disagreed. Neville defended Barrott's decision and tore into Yoro for a "clumsy" challenge and "poor anticipation".

Keane, however, felt Yoro could feel hard done by. He was "amazed" to see De Ligt fail to read the flight of the ball, arguing that Isak's heavy touch meant he wasn't going to regain possession:

"Isak has done well, but I actually don't think he's going to get the ball... He's not getting the ball. There's no way he's getting the ball. It's clumsy from United, it's one straight ball over the top. You shouldn't be in so much trouble, but there's no way he's getting that ball. Absolutely not."

Manchester United legend was less than impressed after the final whistle against Liverpool

This is the second time this season Yoro has been sent off, compounding a miserable afternoon for United. Their loss to Liverpool leaves them sitting 19th after 11 games played, and they will be without the Frenchman for Brentford's visit to Old Trafford on Saturday unless they successfully appeal the decision.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Rio Ngumoha smashes Liverpool record in Manchester United win amid Cristiano Ronaldo comparison

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Rio Ngumoha played a starring role as Liverpool thrashed Manchester United 4-0 on Sunday to hand Mauricio Pochettino a disastrous start to his reign at Old Trafford.

The Reds were on fire at Anfield, with Dominik Szoboszlai, Florian Wirtz, Ngumoha and Victor Munoz all on target for Andoni Iraola’s men, who secured a second straight win.

It followed Leny Yoro’s eighth-minute red card after denying Alexander Isak a clear goalscoring opportunity, as Pochettino watched on in horror from the sidelines.

The Argentine made unwanted history on his debut, becoming the first Premier League manager to suffer conceding two goals and receiving a red card in the first half of his first game in charge.

There was more positive news for the hosts, however, and more specifically, their teenage sensation on the left wing.

Ngumoha, who has caught the eye of England manager Thomas Tuchel with his performances, became the youngest goalscorer in the historic fixture, overtaking Michael Owen as he slotted home the third goal, and he also became the youngest Liverpool player since Owen in 1997 ever to score and assist in a Premier League match.

There is another historic figure whose company he has joined in the history books too.

Now, at just 18 years and 85 days, Ngumoha is in the company of United legend Cristiano Ronaldo, after he became just the second teenager to create three or more chances in a Liverpool vs Man United fixture since records began back in the 2003-04 season.

Before he was substituted late on by Iraola, replaced by fellow goalscorer Munoz, he had registered more touches than any other player on the pitch with 48, and he had twice as many successful dribbles than anyone else (four), terrorising Diogo Dalot down the Liverpool left.

Chivu: ‘Inter must pick up where we left off, important to find balance’

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cristian Chivu outlines his Inter changes for the trip to Atalanta, warning they must ‘pick up where we left off’ with the victory over Como.

It kicks off at the New Balance Arena at 19.45 GMT (20.45 CET).

You can follow all the build-up and action as it happens from today’s five Serie A games on the Liveblog.

The Nerazzurri are feeling the pressure with this schedule, knowing that the other teams fighting for the top spots already secured victories this weekend.

“We all know how Maurizio Sarri plays, his organisation is something he is trying to bring to this team, but has only been here for three or four months, and had a lot of problems with injuries, so was unable to use his ideal defence,” Chivu told DAZN Italia.

“They’ve had a little more time on the break for international duty, even if like us they had a lot of players around the world.

“It is never an easy game here, as both teams have high expectations, so we must build on what we did two weeks ago.”

Inter went into the break for international duty with a victory over Como and Cesc Fabregas, consolidating second place behind surprise outfit Lazio.

Curtis Jones has been out since his injury against Roma on September 19, while Lautaro Martinez and John Stones also return for a spot on the bench.

However, Alessandro Bastoni is picked in the centre of a three-man defence, a role he has sometimes struggled with. Is this an experiment for Chivu?

“I do not perform experiments. I deal with a team and the problems that emerge on a daily basis. Benjamin Pavard has been training well and recovering, Jones is returning slowly, so this is my choice for tonight,” explained the Inter coach.

“We have great players at our disposal, and so many deserve to start, but nowadays the substitutes give a side so much too.”

Ange-Yoan Bonny and Aleksandar Stankovic are still on the absentee list, after missing international duty.

“We’ve got to pick up where we left off against Como, to show our form, fitness, and strategy, because we often talk about names, but we must also see the characteristics, if someone is more attacking or defensive, and provide solutions that also give us balance.”

Inter have a big Champions League fixture coming up against VfB Stuttgart on Wednesday, and absolute must-win after a difficult start to the tournament.

Mauricio Pochettino's Manchester United reign began in humiliating fashion as Liverpool put four past their bitter rivals at Anfield.

Pochettino's game-plan was in tatters inside 15 minutes when Leny Yoro was shown a straight red card and goals from Dominik Szoboszlai and Florian Wirtz took the game away from United.

Even after things settled down, following the introduction of Harry Maguire, United never looked capable of getting back in the game and eventually the worst defence in Europe cost them two more goals.

Rio Ngumoha, the best player on the pitch, converted after a blistering break from Bradley Barcola to make it 3-0.

Victor Munoz, sent on from the bench, added the cherry on top for Liverpool when he buried a fourth late on.

Daily Mail Sport's LEWIS STEELE and NATHAN SALT run the rule over both sets of players at Anfield...

Rio Ngumoha ran Manchester United ragged in his best display in a Liverpool shirt

Mauricio Pochettino learned in a brutal 90 minutes the size of the job he has taken on

A fans' favourite who wears his heart on his sleeve, activating the option to make the Barcelona loanee's stay permanent should be a foregone conclusion.

The big French defender looks like he has been playing alongside Virgil van Dijk for years and is a major upgrade on Ibrahima Konate.

Seemed to enjoy the contest and why wouldn't he? The captain had a quiet afternoon with little to do which, at 35, is not the worst thing ever.

Mauricio Pochettino was immediately up against it in his first few days at this slow-motion car crash of a club - Man United's 4-0 loss at Liverpool showed how much he has to do: OLIVER HOLT

📅 2026-11-22  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 14:08 EST, 22 November 2026 | Updated: 14:52 EST, 22 November 2026

A drone spied on his first training session, his star striker stayed up into the early hours obsessing over Grand Theft Auto the day he arrived, his best player has paused contract talks, his chief executive praised the Glazers and Sir Jim Ratcliffe, and the club banned four supporters for three years for throwing a tennis ball on to the pitch.

That was before the football started. And the fates had decreed that Mauricio Pochettino’s first game in charge as head coach of this misused, abused, diminished, fallen slow-motion car crash of a football club that was once Manchester United but which has lost all sense of its place in the modern football firmament was to be away to Liverpool at Anfield.

And then Pochettino picked a centre-half, Matthijs de Ligt, who had not started a Premier League game for more than a year. And De Ligt began the game by watching a long ball sail over his head as if it were a rare curiosity to be observed rather than to be dealt with and before you could blink, Leny Yoro had been sent off trying to clear up De Ligt’s mess.

So at least Pochettino got to see United set a new record. Yoro was sent off after seven minutes and 23 seconds and that has never been done more quickly by United in the league. So there was that. De Ligt went on, it is sad to report, to have such a dreadful game that it was tempting to watch it through your fingers. Not the best selection decision by the new boss.

And in the stands, Omar Berrada, the strangely bullish United chief executive, looked colder and colder and more and more pale in the chill and, beside him, Jason Wilcox, the director of football, slid deeper and deeper into his big coat until he nearly disappeared from view. Which, presumably, was what he was hoping to do.

Mauricio Pochettino was given a near-impossible task in his first game as manager of Manchester United

Leny Yoro (right) was sent off just minutes into their 4-0 defeat by rivals Liverpool at Anfield

The Reds dominated from then on, with teenage winger Rio Ngumoha one of the Reds' stars

And in the row behind them, Sir Alex Ferguson gazed down on what remained of the great club he had built. James Nesbitt, the esteemed actor who is Ferguson’s friend, sat next to him in the directors’ box. One of Nesbitt’s best performances was in The Missing. The United defence were starring in the remake.

And the Liverpool fans loved it. ‘You’re going down with the City,’ they sang at the United fans. ‘You’re getting sacked in the morning,’ they sang at Pochettino, as he stood, arms tightly folded on the touchline. When it got to 4-0, the Kop broke out another ditty. ‘Poch is at the wheel,’ they sang over and over again.

So, welcome to Manchester, Mauricio. Welcome to the sight of United consolidating their position one off the bottom of the Premier League. If they keep this going for another week, they will be in the bottom three of the top flight in December for the first time in more than 50 years. If Pochettino did not know it before, he knows it now: there is an awful lot of saving to be done on the red side of Manchester.

This is a relegation fight that United are in. Don’t kid yourself that it’s not. The last time they dropped out of the top flight in 1973-74, they won three games in their first 11 matches. This season, they have won just once in the first 11 and that was at home to Ipswich Town on August 30th. A lot of the teams around them have got something about them, too. They certainly have more spirit than this rabble.

In that context, it was not the best omen that this was the worst result in a United manager’s first game for more than 100 years. If Pochettino thought getting the USA to win the World Cup was a big ask, rebuilding United is up there with it. This is a club mired in dysfunction on and off the pitch. It is a Herculean task to rescue them and we are about to find out whether Pochettino has it in him.

It was strange seeing a new United manager anointed at Anfield as the crowd sang You’ll Never Walk Alone and the Kop flew its flags and the faces of Shankly, Paisley, Dalglish, Klopp and the rest stared down from the banner of all the bosses here who have won league titles. Pochettino stood on the sideline and drank it all in, right until kick off.

It was his misfortune that this is also a Liverpool team beginning to click. Florian Wirtz is beginning to express himself and that is a wonderful sight. Bradley Barcola and Alexander Isak are becoming a formidable partnership. Rio Ngumoha scared Diogo Dalot rigid. Liverpool are rising again as United flounder.

It did not take long for Pochettino to get his first look at the uselessness that has afflicted United for most of this season. Only seven minutes had gone when Ronald Araujo drove a searching ball over the top of the United defence. De Ligt watched it go and abrogated responsibility for it, expecting Yoro to cover. Isak sprinted after it and by the time Yoro finally reacted, it was too late. Isak was on to it.

Pochettino opted to start Matthijs de Ligt (right) - but he struggled throughout the game

He knows the size of the task of saving United - and whether he can do it will determine his legacy as a manager

Liverpool, meanwhile, are starting to click - and Florian Wirtz is at the centre of that rise

Isak got ahead of Yoro and pushed the ball forwards. It was a heavy touch and would have run on to Lammens but Yoro compounded his error by bundling Isak down on the edge of the area. He was sent off by referee Sam Barrott. Only seven minutes and 23secs had elapsed. It was the quickest sending off in United’s Premier League history. It was not a controversy. It was just a show of defensive incompetence.

A free-kick in that kind of position might as well be a penalty when Dominik Szoboszlai is standing over it. He lashed it low and hard past the edge of the United wall. Bruno Fernandes shook a half-interested leg at it. It whistled past him and bulged the corner of the United net.

Liverpool (4-2-3-1): Alisson; Araujo (Gomez 85), Jacquet, van Dijk, Kerkez; Szoboszlai, Mac Allister (Nyoni 72); Barcola (Munoz 72), Wirtz, Ngumoha (Frimpong 78); Isak (Koumas 85)

Subs not used: Mamardashvili, Tsimikas, McConnell, Chiesa

Goals: Szoboszlai 10, Wirtz 13, Ngumoha 66, Munoz 82

Man United (4-2-3-1): Lammens; Dalot, Yoro, De Ligt, Martinez; Tielemans (Mount 65), Mainoo (Santos 87); Mbeumo (Maguire 22), Fernandes, Dorgu (Mazraoui 65); Cunha (Zirkzee 46)

Subs not used: Heaton, Heaven, Diallo, Lacey

Three minutes later, Liverpool were further ahead. Ngumoha embarrassed Dalot down the Liverpool left – not for the last time – and pulled a cross back for Wirtz. It was just behind the Germany playmaker but Wirtz has beautiful technique and somehow, he hooked it goalwards with his left foot and it crashed in off the underside of the bar.

United were in chaos. Pochettino looked stunned. He brought on Harry Maguire for Grand Theft Auto aficionado Bryan Mbeumo after 22 minutes. Maybe it was a bitter pill for Mbeumo. Maybe it will have just allowed him to spend more time with his PlayStation. Practice makes perfect, after all.

And so United spent the rest of the half repelling a siege of their goal. Fernandes summoned one shot on target but it dribbled apologetically through to Alisson from the edge of the area. Most of the time, though, it was like the Alamo at the United end. Maguire made one particularly heroic block from Isak.

Liverpool should have gone three-nil up just before the hour. Wirtz played a fine through ball to Barcola, who ran clear of the defence. Barcola had only Lammens to beat but instead of slotting the ball past him, he tried to dribble around him, got his feet mixed up and kicked the ball out of play.

There was frustration at Anfield. They wanted more than this. This was a chance to rub United’s noses in it, to inflict more misery on their hapless rivals. And yet, there was a sense they were letting them off the hook.

That feeling deepened when De Ligt made a dreadful mess of intercepting a lofted pass to Isak, missed his kick and fell over. Isak raced through but then blasted his shot hopelessly high and wide. Then Barcola turned De Ligt and hit another shot too high.

Liverpool could not keep missing all afternoon, though, and they finally got their third when Barcola skipped past Lisandro Martinez on the halfway line as if he wasn’t there and sprinted down the right. He squared the ball across the box to Ngumoha and Ngumoha slammed it unerringly past Lammens. Victor Munoz drilled home the fourth eight minutes from time and United’s humiliation was complete.

Ange Postecoglou snaps at reporter as he defends Sadio Mane for missing match to be at birth of child - after defeat which saw Cristiano Ronaldo fail to score a penalty

📅 2026-11-22  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 11:10 EST, 22 November 2026 | Updated: 04:10 EST, 23 November 2026

Footage has emerged of Ange Postecoglou locked in a fiery exchange with a reporter after Al-Nassr's defeat to Al-Ahli - after he questioned Sadio Mane's commitment to the Saudi Pro League club.

The side sits third in the league standings, but were humbled in the 3-1 loss to their fourth-placed rivals on the road.

Mane was not named in the matchday squad despite registering two assists in the club's last outing, a 6-2 victory against Asian Champions League opponents Pakhtakor FC.

One Saudi journalist is believed to have used Mane's absence to suggest that he was not sufficiently engaged with Al-Nassr's campaign this season.

But even the suggestion was enough to incense the former Tottenham head coach, who immediately asked if the journalist was 'a father?'

'Do you have children?' he queried, with the journalist responding that he did not.

Ange Postecoglou snapped at the journalist who questioned the strength of Sadio Mane's commitment to Al-Nassr

Should footballers ever be criticised for choosing family over club commitments?

The Senegal forward missed the match as he and his wife await the birth of their second child

'That's probably why you ask the question,' Postecoglou hit back. 'Because when you do, you'll realise it's the most important thing, the biggest blessing you can have.

'(Mane) is with his wife while she is having a baby, OK? Nothing is more important in life.

'Yes, he had my permission, my blessing, and Sadio Mane is one of the best people in this world, mate.

'Never question him or his commitment to this football club.'

Mane welcomed his first child with wife Aisha Timba last year, after marrying the then-18-year-old in January 2024.

In his first match following the happy news, Mane scored and celebrated by rocking an imaginary baby in his arms.

Postecoglou and Mane have only worked with one another since the beginning of the season, but appear to have formed a strong bond with Mane a frequent starter in the Riyadh-based starting XI.

The Antipodean manager joined the club following a spell without work after his ignominious departure from Nottingham Forest last season.

Since arriving in the Gulf state, Postecoglou has overseen a strong domestic campaign, with Al-Nassr registering just one previous defeat this season, against Al-Ettifaq.

But Friday's loss may have stung all the more after Al-Nassr initially led against their bitter rivals.

Cristiano Ronaldo had opened the scoring for the visitors on Friday evening in Jeddah, but missed a penalty striking the far post and failed to double his side's lead.

Instead, it was Ivan Toney who was nerveless from the spot as he netted to put Al-Ahli 2-1 up in the 71st minute, with Roger Ibanez providing the winner four minutes later.

Liverpool vs Manchester United - Premier League: All the reaction as calamitous Red Devils are thrashed 4-0 by bitter rivals in Mauricio Pochettino's first game in charge

📅 2026-11-22  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By HARRY BAMFORTH, LIVE SPORTS COVERAGE REPORTER

Follow Daily Mail Sport's live coverage for the latest updates from Anfield as Mauricio Pochettino takes charge of Manchester United for the first time at rivals Liverpool in the Premier League.

If you couldn't tell already, these are dark days for Manchester United.

I know, I know, we haven't stopped going on about it. I mean, it's hard after watching them get pumped by their biggest rivals away from home. Oh yeah, and they're in the relegation zone in November...

United will end the day in 19th position in the Premier League, with this the first time since November 1986 that they have been within the bottom two places of the league table having played at least 10 matches (21st in that case).

Pochettino needs to change things quickly, very quickly.

That is the chant that will be going through Andoni Iraola's head tonight; he just loves playing Manchester United.

Iraola just never loses to United; he is now unbeaten in all of his seven Premier League matches against Manchester United. That was his third win in that run, too.

The only manager to record a longer unbeaten run against the Red Devils in the competition is one of his predecessors, Jurgen Klopp (eight between 2018 and 2022).

Liverpool certainly picked the right man for this rivalry.

Liverpool's 4-0 win over Man Utd showed just how much Poch has to do

OLIVER HOLT AT ANFIELD: Omar Berrada, the United chief executive, looked colder and colder in the chill and, beside him, Jason Wilcox, the director of football, slid deeper and deeper into his coat.

I reckon Liverpool fans will have been all over this already, while Manchester United supporters will be steering well clear of YouTube.

But anyway, the highlights are out, and it's my job to share them with all of you.

Liverpool love battering Manchester United in the modern era, don't they?

I mean, they seem to do it every couple of seasons...

Since the start of the 2021-22 season, Liverpool have four wins by four or more goals against Manchester United. Don't forget they beat them 3-0 under Arne Slot at Old Trafford during their title-winning season, too.

Those four wins are the most any Premier League team has achieved against another across all competitions.

And what makes it sweeter is that it's against their biggest rivals.

It's time to give Florian Wirtz his props.

Plenty of people have been quick to hammer Wirtz when he is off it for Liverpool because of his price tag; now the same people need to start taking their hats off to him.

That was Wirtz's best performance in a Liverpool shirt, and you can't say it hasn't been coming. He's been improving game by game for weeks.

It felt like almost everything Liverpool did well went through Wirtz today; you could see the lad was brimming with confidence from minute one. And well, that shows by him bagging a goal and an assist.

Wirtz created 10 chances in open play against Manchester United, the most by a player in a Premier League match since David Silva against West Brom in March 2015.

Now the German's coming good; it's looking scary for the league.

Andoni Iraola, Liverpool boss, speaking to Sky Sports:

At the end of the match, the Liverpool fans were asking their boss to go full Jurgen Klopp-style and give them some fist bumps, just like in the old days.

Arne Slot never obliged when asked, but a beaming Iraola was happy to give it big to the Kop in his own unique way. There was something very wholesome about it all.

The Liverpool fans are starting to really take to their new head coach; the bond between them is being built.

This feels much more like the Liverpool of old.

Just when you think Manchester United can't sink any lower, they somehow manage to.

I thought the season they finished 15th under Ruben Amorim is the worst United team we would see this century, but I think this bunch is even worse.

Accounting for three points for a win all-time, United’s eight points this season are their fewest after 11 games of a league campaign since 1972-73 (7).

That is also the only previous season in which the Red Devils only managed one win through their first 11 league matches.

Things are looking pretty damn bleak at Old Trafford.

‘Give him the Heisman’: Opposing coach praises Jeremiah Smith after Ohio State loss

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Nebraska picked up the biggest win of Matt Rhule's tenure on Saturday, beating No. 14 Ohio State 31-28 in Lincoln and likely ending the Buckeyes' College Football Playoff hopes. But even after the win, Rhule had plenty of praise for the opponent's biggest star.

Rhule said that Ohio State wide receiver Jeremiah Smith should win the Heisman Trophy, and that he's never competed against a player as "talented and special."

Nebraska jumped out to a 21-7 lead in the second quarter, but Ohio State battled back to tie the game at 21 in the third quarter. The Huskers then pulled away in the fourth quarter on a 33-yard field goal and a 26-yard touchdown pass, before holding on after a late Buckeyes touchdown.

Smith did his part, finishing with 165 receiving yards and two touchdowns, including a 67-yard score on Ohio State's first drive. But the Buckeyes managed just 76 rushing yards, while Nebraska ran for 176.

The loss drops Ohio State to 7-4, and it's the Buckeyes' latest setback in a frustrating season. Smith drew criticism earlier this month when one longtime Ohio State reporter called him "a petulant child" for his frustration on the sideline during the team's loss to Oregon.

For Nebraska, which also improved to 7-4, it was the program's first win over Ohio State since 2011.

While the Buckeyes might have lost the game, Rhule didn't hesitate to praise Ohio State's biggest during his on-field interview with Fox's Jenny Taft after the game.

"Jeremiah Smith, give him the Heisman. I mean, we doubled him," Rhule said.

He doubled down during his postgame press conference.

"I don't think I've ever competed against a player as talented and special — and I've had a chance to play against a lot of them — than that kid Jeremiah Smith. That man, he is unbelievable, the way he can absolutely just tilt a game," Rhule said.

Smith is widely considered the top player in college football. While Ohio State has fallen out of the College Football Playoff conversation in recent weeks, Smith has remained at or near the top of the Heisman Trophy odds. Rhule seems to think it would be well-deserved if he took home the hardware next month.

Simeone thrilled with Atletico’s position despite lack of goals from strikers

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Diego Simeone has called for perspective amid concerns over the form of Atletico Madrid's strikers, insisting he is "excited" by his side's progress despite their lack of goals.

Ahead of Sunday's Madrid derby against Getafe at the Estadio Coliseum, Simeone reiterated his confidence in his forwards, pointing to the contributions of Atletico's other attacking players as a reason for optimism.

Indeed, Julian Alvarez, Jonathan David and Alexander Sorloth have combined for just four LaLiga goals between them this season, leaving much of the goalscoring burden on Atletico's wide players, with Alex Baena (five goals), Giuliano Simeone (four) and Alejandro Grimaldo (four) leading the way.

Despite their strikers' struggles, Atletico sit third in LaLiga, five points behind second-placed Real Madrid and leaders Barcelona.

"On the goals, it's all about perspective," Simeone said. "I'm excited that we're where we are without the strikers scoring.

"Hopefully, the goals will come, and then we can build on all the good things we're doing.

"We're going to face Getafe, who are a very tough opponent on their home turf. We need to play a strong game and take the match to where we can hurt them.

"We need to maintain what we've been doing and continue managing our playing time so that we can show our ability to compete at the level we need to."

The Atletico boss also provided updates on defenders Robin Le Normand and Cristian Romero, both of whom have featured regularly for Atletico this season.

"We have a lot of confidence in [Le Normand]," Simeone added.

"We have a very good relationship, we often talk about what's been happening to him. In football, mistakes are more damaging to defenders, but with his personality he'll do well.

"Cuti Romero also is working very hard to recover as quickly as possible. Even without playing, he's very important. We need the best Cuti Romero."

Getafe, meanwhile, are 15th ahead of Sunday's kick-off, though Jose Bordalas' side remain unbeaten at home in the league this season (W3 D3), and have not suffered defeat across their last eight LaLiga matches.

— Atlético de Madrid (@atletienglish) November 21, 2026

Enes Unal has scored five goals in 12 games against Atletico in LaLiga – his highest tally against a single opponent in the top-flight.

Lee Kang-In has found the net three times in eight LaLiga outings against Getafe.

Against no other opponent has he scored more goals across his career in Europe's top five leagues, along with three against Angers for Paris Saint-Germain in Ligue 1.

Atletico head into this clash as favourites, having won 10 of their 13 away matches against Getafe in LaLiga (D2 L1).

Los Colchoneros have not managed more away wins against any other opponent in the league (also 10 against Celta Vigo).

Simeone's side also arrive in strong away form, having won their last four LaLiga matches on the road. They have only managed five or more consecutive away victories in a single campaign on two previous occasions – in April 2014 and February 2021, both of which were title-winning seasons.

Simeone himself boasts an impressive record against Getafe, having won 22 of his 27 LaLiga meetings with them (D4 L1). Victory on Sunday would see him equal Luis Aragones' record for the most wins by a manager against a single opponent in the competition, with the latter having beaten Espanyol 23 times.

Getafe, though, have taken points from three of their last seven league meetings with Atleti (W1 D2 L4), having done so in just two of the previous 20 (D2 L18).

Bordalas' side will also take confidence from their recent form at the Coliseum, where they are unbeaten in eight LaLiga matches (W5 D3) – their longest such run since going 11 games without defeat between May and December 2023 (W7 D4).

Arteta sweating on Raya’s fitness after Arsenal’s thriller with Newcastle

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Mikel Arteta was left sweating over the fitness of goalkeeper David Raya after the Arsenal shot-stopper was forced off in Saturday's 2-2 draw against Newcastle United.

Raya was replaced in the 70th minute by Kepa Arrizabalaga at St. James' Park after clutching his left arm and looking in some discomfort after a challenge with William Osula.

By the time Raya was replaced, Arsenal found themselves 2-1 down after goals from Harvey Barnes and Malick Thiaw overturned Eberechi Eze's 33rd-minute opener.

However, Arsenal hit back 12 minutes from time through Noni Madueke, ensuring they remained six points clear of second-place Brighton at the top of the Premier League.

Arteta, however, remained concerned about the fitness of his goalkeeper, who has kept the joint-most clean sheets (seven, level with Bart Verbruggen) in the league this season.

The Gunners face Borussia Dortmund in the Champions League on Tuesday before welcoming Enzo Maresca's Manchester City to the Emirates Stadium five days later.

"Unfortunately, he had to come off, which is never good news, and he will get assessed now," Arteta said of Raya's injury.

"He will probably get an MRI scan tomorrow, to see what exactly it is. At the moment, I don't know."

Arsenal's result saw them end a 21-game run of Premier League games without a draw (W18 D3) since a 2-2 draw with Wolves back in February.

Backed throughout a resilient away display Safe travels home, Gooners https://t.co/PvE3TEdYPZ

In an entertaining affair in the second half, Arsenal were edged in the expected goals (xG) battle, registering a total of 1.48 to Newcastle's 1.73 from one fewer attempt (12 to 13).

Newcastle had just three shots (0 on target), one touch in the opposition’s box, and won 46% of their duels in the first half, while facing 11 shots themselves (three on target).

In the second half, they had nine shots (three on target), 14 touches in the opposition’s box, and won 52.5% of their duels, facing just two shots from Arsenal (one on target).

And Arteta was left to rue a glut of chances while Arsenal were on top in the first half, conceding: "My feeling is that we have lost two points today. I say 'we', because we were exceptional in the first half.

"The level of aggression and intensity we put into the defensive aspect, the quality we showed with the clarity to understand the jumps and the way they were making every adjustment man-to-man, the aggression to attack the right spaces, and we should have been three or four up.

"When you don't do that, then in the second half, you know the opponents will try to react, and that’s what they did to us.

"You have to increase the level to cope with that, rather than stay at the same level. Right from the beginning, we're not at the races – we start to lose duels, start to lose second balls, we give the ball away and have really bad circulation balls against their press, and we give them belief.

"We did both, giving them a goal from a throw-in that was very disappointing, and then we gave them a corner, and left their best set-piece player free – we get punished.

"After that, we came back to being that exceptional team; we could break the press, run, create infinite situations where we could score a goal, we scored the second one, and we had infinite situations with overloads to go and kill the game and win it.

"We lacked that moment of magic to win the game in the end."

‘That was Newcastle DNA-par excellence’ – Jaissle delighted after Arsenal draw

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Matthias Jaissle believes he saw "Newcastle United DNA-par excellence" in their second-half display against Arsenal.

The Magpies held the Gunners to a 2-2 draw at St. James' Park on Saturday, after recovering from a dismal first-half performance.

After Eberechi Eze gave Arsenal a 1-0 lead at the break, Harvey Barnes and Malick Thiaw turned things around for Newcastle, though Noni Madueke salvaged a draw for the reigning champions.

Newcastle had just three shots (none on target), one touch in the opposition’s box, and won 46% of their duels during the first half, while facing 11 shots themselves (three on target).

In the second half, they had nine shots (three on target), 14 touches in the opposition box, and won 52.5% of their duels, facing just two shots from Arsenal (one on target).

Barnes' equaliser was the first goal the Gunners have conceded when leading in the Premier League this season, and Jaissle was delighted with how his players responded after the break.

"We played in the second-half exactly how we want to play football."  Matthias Jaissle on this evening's 2-2 draw with Arsenal at St. James' Park https://t.co/eQzA6p3wuf

— Newcastle United (@NUFC) November 21, 2026

"Mixed feelings," he said. "Like the first home match here against Liverpool, where a little bit more was possible.

"We also need to be realistic and humble. The first half was not good. The second half was excellent, so if you combine these two then I think a point is fair.

"Second half, the momentum and the energy in the stadium, you get the feeling that a little bit more is possible.

"We encouraged them to do the high press in the right way, with a front-footed approach, not hesitating, not doubting, just pure belief in the way we want to do it.

"You saw that in the second half, that was Newcastle DNA-par excellence. A point against the best team in the Premier League is something we can build on."

District 5-2A champ North Star faces big state playoff test in Westinghouse

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

North Star High School football coach Shane Smith made a straightforward observation about the Westinghouse team his Cougars will face in the PIAA Class 2A playoffs Saturday.

"This is a machine like we haven't seen all year long," Smith said of the undefeated and state sixth-ranked Bulldogs. "They are very big and very fast. On tape, they look like a very physical football team.

"We definitely have to play the best game we've played all year long."

Kickoff is at noon at Bedford High School.

North Star (10-2) won its first District 5 title in 15 years Nov. 13, defeating Berlin Brothersvalley 16-7 in the Class 2A title game at Somerset Area High School.

District 8 Westinghouse (10-0), the Pittsburgh City League champion, has outscored opponents 489-81, an average of 48.9 to 8.1 points a game.

The Bulldogs beat Karns City 50-21 Nov. 13, marking the third straight game Westinghouse scored exactly 50 points. Coach Donta Green's squad tallied a season-high 81 points against Brashear and followed that with 66 points against Taylor Allderdice.

"We're going to go in with the best game plan possible, but we know the test that lies ahead of us," Smith said. "We are going to have to play very disciplined, fundamental football."

Westinghouse quarterback Omar Pack ran for 170 yards and scored three touchdowns against the Gremlins Nov. 13.

Running back Jordan Reid's 131-yard rushing performance in the subregional helped the Bulldogs collect 413 rushing yards.

"We have to tackle better than we have all year long," Smith said. "Their team speed is just off the charts. If we give them a little crease, it's going to go to the house.

"We have to match their physicality. Fundamental, physical football is going to be the approach this week."

North Star is led by senior Kasey Yoder, fourth in the area with 1,413 passing yards, 22 touchdowns and eight interceptions. In the District 5-2A title game, Yoder carried 17 times for 94 yards, including a touchdown. He only threw three passes for a net of two yards.

On defense, Yoder closed the game with a 45-yard interception return to the end zone.

Junior back Will Eshleman ran for 85 yards on 15 carries against Berlin Brothersvalley, as North Star earned its first District 5 championship since a repeat title performance in 2011.

"We had a big win last week – first District 5 (title) in a number of years," Smith said. "We're going to run with the momentum we have. We've had a good week of practice. The attitude is upbeat and positive."

Westinghouse has had success against District 5 opponents, winning in the playoffs five consecutive seasons.

In 2025, the Bulldogs beat Meyersdale (44-0) in the Class 1A playoffs after previously earning 2A wins over Windber (2021, 2024) and Berlin Brothersvalley (2022, 2023).

Westinghouse reached the Class 2A state final in back-to-back seasons, falling each time to Southern Columbia in the 2022 (37-22) and 2023 (21-20) championship games.

Prior to the current stretch, the Bulldogs struggled against District 5, dropping seven straight playoff games from 2013-19 against Bedford (2018, 2019), Berlin Brothersvalley (2016, 2017) and Chestnut Ridge (2013, 2014, 2015).

"We're going to rely on Kasey Yoder, and we're hoping Will Eshleman has a nice game for us," Smith said. "Westinghouse's team fronts are very big. This will be the first time our guys will be tested by somebody bigger than us.

"I'm anxious to see how our big guys perform as the 'little' guys this week."

Mike Mastovich is a sports reporter and columnist for The Tribune-Democrat. He can be reached at 814-532-5083. Follow him on Twitter @Masty81.

MVG still a force to be reckoned with ahead of Grand Slam semi-finals

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Debutant Kevin Doets faces three-time champion Gerwyn Price in the first semi-final before Michael van Gerwen and Gian van Veen renew their rivalry on the big stage; watch the climax of the Grand Slam of Darts in Wolverhampton live on Sky Sports

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Michael van Gerwen has shown he is still a force to be reckoned with as he prepares to face fellow Dutchman Gian van Veen in his first major semi-final in over a year.

With Luke Littler and Luke Humphries already at home, Van Gerwen will be looking to get his hands on some TV silverware for the first time since the 2025 World Series of Darts Finals.

Van Gerwen, who threw perfection in the opening leg against Tom Sykes to record his first televised nine-darter on Sky Sports in 14 years, takes on Van Veen on Sunday, with debutant Kevin Doets going head-to-head with three-time champion Gerwyn Price in the first semi-final.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"There has been a lot of criticism about me in the last couple of years, and rightly so, because I wasn't performing. Then you need to make sure you pull your finger out," said Van Gerwen, who traded nine-darters with Sykes on a historic night in Wolverhampton.

"To reach the semi-final is nice, but then to also do it the way I did it today.

"This means the world to me. It has been a battle; I have to work hard, I know that, but then to also show it, it's magnificent.

"I have been feeling good, and to also produce what I am doing, I hope I can do even more later in the tournament. I am feeling good, and when I feel good, I can do anything; I know that.

"I showed some real class and some real ability, and moving on that gives me goosebumps, that gives me the energy, and I hope I can show more tomorrow."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Van Veen defeated 2022 runner-up Nathan Aspinall in a bruising battle to set up a meeting against his Dutch compatriot.

"As a Dutchman, a Grand Slam semi-final against Michael is always a dream coming true, but as you said, the way he played today, it was phenomenal," admitted the former European champion.

"If he does that on Sunday, I'm going to be in a whole lot of trouble, but hopefully he doesn't, and hopefully I'll step up my game and then it's going to be a totally different game and very close.

"Michael played phenomenal. Fair play to him, but as I said, it's going to be a totally different game on Sunday and hopefully I'll be ready.

"I will put 100 per cent focus in that semi-final and then as soon as I hopefully get that job done, then we will worry about the final.

"If you worry already about the final before the semi, you might lose quite convincingly."

Three-time world champion John Part speaking on Sky Sports:

"Michael has handled Gian well this year. He hasn't dropped a match to him. There's been nothing really hugely consequential about any of the matches they played.

"Michael's going to be feeling really good and that's the problem for Gian.

"He's going to have to come out, grind into the match, but he'll hopefully want to spark into the flowing, really better average stuff, the A-game type stuff.

"That was pretty good quality overall for the length of format tonight, but he'll think, ' oh, I can do so much better'. And he'll want to. And against Michael, who better to do it against."

Price will take on Doets with the Welshman into an eighth successive televised semi-final at the PDC this year, although he has yet to break his duck and is without a major victory since 2022.

'The Iceman' is chasing a fourth Grand Slam title and first in five years, but Dutchman Doets is enjoying a brilliant breakout season, claiming his first senior PDC title at Players Championship 13 in May before reaching his maiden European Tour final at the Austrian Darts Open.

He powered past Jermaine Wattimena to reach his first televised PDC semi-final, with his red-hot form impressing Wayne Mardle.

"Kevin Doets won 11 of the last 13 legs against an opponent averaging a ton. That's how good he is," said Mardle.

"I feel really strongly about Kevin Doets' future.

"It wouldn't surprise me if he deals with Gerwyn Price and goes on and wins it.

"He's playing so well so often, and you've got to play good enough often enough, and he does.

Zlatan Ibrahimovic Named English Icon He Wished Played in Europe

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Over the years, Zlatan Ibrahimovic earned himself a fearsome reputation. On the pitch, he was never afraid to clash with anyone – including teammates and managers.

In retirement, as a pundit, he's been just as sharp-tongued when appropriate. However, that doesn't mean he's afraid to dish out deserved praise. Indeed, the Swedish icon – who won league titles with Ajax, Inter Milan, Barcelona, AC Milan, and Paris Saint-Germain across his distinguished career – is known for occasionally recognising greatness.

During his playing days, he actually expressed disappointment that one 'fantastic' icon, who just so happened to be one of the best English footballers of all time, never left to play for a club in Europe.

Zlatan felt Ronaldo 'took all the glory' for himself.

The man in question is Steven Gerrard. The Liverpool legend will forever be remembered as one of the greatest midfielders in football history. Most famously, he won the Champions League with the Reds and also earned 114 caps with England.

While many European giants would have loved to have signed Gerrard, he remained committedto Liverpool during his prime years. Ibrahimovic lamented this, once claiming he wanted to see the Englishman play abroad.

In 2012, ahead of the Three Lions' clash with Sweden, with Gerrard set to win his 100th cap, Zlatan spoke about how much respect he had for the midfielder:

"He's a fantastic player, always someone who plays at a high level. He's a good fighter and has good skills also and is strong with the ball.

"He feels more like an international player than a normal English player. Whenever I play against Steven the coach always says, 'Be careful with that player because he can make the difference.'

"He is respected throughout Europe but I would like to see Steven in, of course Liverpool is a big club, but in a big international club. I would like to see that also for England because it is very exciting when someone goes abroad and shows who he is in another competition.

"For me a fantastic player can make a difference in every country, wherever he plays, and I'm pretty sure that Steven would do that also. Of course, he is playing in one of the best leagues in the world now but he's a fantastic player."

Ibrahimovic was also asked to clarify what he considers a 'typical English player' to be, the then-PSG forward replied:

"Someone with a big heart, always fighting, aggressive, who never gives up. For me Steven Gerrard has more international skills than a normal player. England are used to playing 4-4-2 and the big teams in the Premier League are playing 4-3-3 with a lot of foreign players. It is developing like every other competition and every other country. They are a fantastic team with fantastic players."

Sadly for Ibrahimovic, and many football fans, the Swede didn't get his wish. Gerrard would never play for a European club, spending the bulk of his career at Liverpool.

However, the Englishman would leave Anfield in 2015 to spend one season in the United States. Coincidentally, he joined LA Galaxy, the same MLS side Zlatan would also later play for. Their paths did not cross in America, though, with the striker joining Galaxy in 2018, two seasons after Gerrard retired.

Ibrahimovic also came to England for a while, playing for Manchester United from 2016 to 2018. During his time at Old Trafford, he did at least get to share the pitch with another English icon he 'liked more than anyone', Wayne Rooney.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Kicker Smyth elevated to Saints' playing roster

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Kicker Charlie Smyth has been elevated to the New Orleans Saints' playing roster and has signed a new deal with the NFL franchise.

The former gaelic footballer, from Mayobridge in Northern Ireland, has started the last two NFL games for the Saints after being promoted from the practice squad.

The 25-year-old was handed a second opportunity to be the Saints' kicker after Dan Carlson, who started the season as first choice, was released at the start of November due to mixed form.

Smyth helped the Saints to an overtime win over the Cleveland Browns on his return to the team and then kicked a late 55-yard field goal in the 20-17 defeat by the Carolina Panthers on Sunday.

NFL rules state a player from a practice squad cannot be used more than three times in a season, and Smyth has been rewarded for his impressive return.

He made his NFL debut for New Orleans last November but was released by the franchise in August.

Smyth was retained on their practice squad due to having an exemption as he came through the NFL's international player pathway.

His first game since signing his new deal is at the Chicago Bears on Sunday at 18:00 GMT.

The Best is Yet to Come for Caleb Williams and the Bears’ Offense

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Chicago Bears' bye week landed as close to the halfway point of the NFL season as possible, with 17 games on the schedule, giving us an opportunity to check back in with the team's offensive playmakers ahead of this week's matchup with New Orleans. A chaotic first few weeks saw slow starts from ascending pass-catchers Rome Odunze, Luther Burden, and Colston Loveland, absences from Caleb Williams and Kyle Monangai, and worst of all, a season-ending injury to Cole Kmet. Despite the waves created by the acquisition of defensive star Jeffery Simmons, this team was built with Ben Johnson's offensive identity at the forefront. This latest edition of Benchmarking Da Bears will serve as a reminder of just that:

Comp Player: 2022 Jared Goff (Detroit Lions)

Caleb Williams came out of the gate on fire in Carolina, leading the Bears to a 59-point offensive explosion in their 2026 debut. Unfortunately, the following week he faced a torrential downpour, Brian Flores' Vikings defense, and a hamstring injury that would keep him out for three games. Since his return, the Bears have not trailed. That launched Williams into the MVP conversation, and even had him near the front of the crowd until an ugly 4 interception game against Tampa Bay took a little wind out of his sails. Still, Williams has been playing extremely well, and there's reason to believe he can keep up this high-level of play in the latter half of the season.

Comp Player: 2025 D'Andre Swift (Chicago Bears)

One half of the Bears' admired tandem, Swift has been the lone back standing in recent weeks as Kyle Monangai continues to recover from his bout with turf toe. Swift hasn't looked completely healthy, which has reflected in his yards per carry numbers, but he's managed to power through and produced some much-needed scores for Chicago anyway. Swift has already set a new career high in rushing touchdowns (10), with a hat trick in Seattle serving as a monument to his and the offense as a whole's ability to commit to the run game.

Comp Player: 2023 Sam LaPorta (Detroit Lions)

Speaking of injuries, I'd be remiss not to mention the loss of Cole Kmet. Kmet went down for the year in the Bears' Thursday night victory over the New England Patriots, leaving them with gaps to fill in both the blocking and receiving game. It would be a stretch to say they've found a solution yet, but Colston Loveland could help if he's able to find some of the end-of-season form he showed last year. He's started slow in back-to-back seasons; can we expect him to elevate once again?

Comp Player: 2024 Jameson Williams (Detroit Lions)

Comp Player: 2022 Amon-Ra St. Brown (Detroit Lions)

Comp Player: 2022 Kalif Raymond (Detroit Lions)

The Bears' top trio of receivers have taken turns stepping up to the plate this season. Despite a disappointing start, Rome Odunze has shown a lot of flashes in recent weeks, particularly when it matters most. He's helped seal the game against New England and secure an early lead against Tampa Bay – which turned out to be pretty key. While his catch rate and lack of scores are less than ideal, his yardage is there, and as he grows more comfortable, we can hope to see continued improvements from Rome. In similar ways, Raymond started the year off as the Bears' go-to pass-catcher and Burden has had sporadic bursts throughout. If any or all of these three can find some consistency in the latter half of the year, the offense can certainly elevate to a new level at the right time.

The Saints are the first team up after the bye week for Chicago, but after that, they start what has shaped up to be the most intimidating stretch of any teams' schedule this season. In the NFC North, they may need to do exactly what Ben Johnson's preached and play their best football starting in November and December in order to even secure a spot in the playoffs.
